# Wolfram Community: functions for the Wolfram Physics Project

Research report for the WolframInstitute/WolframPhysics guide page. Source: Wolfram Community (https://community.wolfram.com/), swept on 2026-09-16. It lists, per sub-source, the functions that community posts define in code (candidates for the paclet) and the Function Repository and paclet functions those posts rely on. Every name was seen by the finder in a fetched page or a kernel evaluation; names that appear in post code without a visible definition and without a Function Repository entry are marked unconfirmed.

## Method

Wolfram Community moved to a Discourse instance in 2026 (page generator reports Discourse 2026.9.0). Legacy URLs of the form https://community.wolfram.com/groups/-/m/t/NNNNNNN redirect (HTTP 301) to https://community.wolfram.com/t/slug/ID, and the legacy "Wolfram Physics Project" group URL https://community.wolfram.com/content?curTag=wolfram%20physics%20project returns HTTP 404. The old Physics Project group content now lives under the Discourse tag "ruliology" (892 topics), with the school project posts additionally tagged "wolfram-summer-research-institute" and the highlighted ones "staff-pick". Every step below was run on 2026-09-16.

### Web searches (WebSearch tool)

1. site:community.wolfram.com "Wolfram Physics" multiway
2. site:community.wolfram.com hypergraph rewriting
3. site:community.wolfram.com branchial graph
4. site:community.wolfram.com rulial space
5. site:community.wolfram.com "ResourceFunction" "WolframModel"
6. site:community.wolfram.com WSS20 "Wolfram Model" causal graph
7. site:community.wolfram.com WSS21 multiway system
8. site:community.wolfram.com WSS23 hypergraph Wolfram model
9. site:community.wolfram.com WSS22 "Wolfram model" OR multiway OR hypergraph
10. site:community.wolfram.com WSS24 multiway OR hypergraph OR "Wolfram model" OR ruliology
11. site:community.wolfram.com WSS25 multiway OR hypergraph OR "Wolfram model" OR causal
12. site:community.wolfram.com WWS26 multiway OR hypergraph OR causal OR rulial

### Discourse JSON endpoints (fetched with curl and python urllib, User-Agent Mozilla/5.0)

- https://community.wolfram.com/tags.json (tag inventory; found the "ruliology" tag, id 8, 892 topics)
- https://community.wolfram.com/categories.json (categories: announcements, staff-picks, questions, ideas, challenges, jobs, meta)
- https://community.wolfram.com/tag/ruliology.json?page=N for N = 0..29 (all 892 topics in the tag; saved as tag_ruliology.json)
- https://community.wolfram.com/tag/physics.json?page=N and https://community.wolfram.com/tag/graphs-and-networks.json?page=N (full listings, used to look for Physics Project posts not tagged ruliology)
- https://community.wolfram.com/search.json?q=QUERY&page=N (N up to 8) for the queries: WolframModel, MultiwaySystem, hypergraph rewriting, branchial, rulial, ruliad, causal graph, multiway, combinators, SetReplace, TokenEventGraph, WolframInstitute, Hypergraph paclet, multicomputation, Wolfram Physics Project, causal invariance, string substitution system, MultiwayTuringMachine, MultiwayDeterministicTuringMachine, HypergraphPlot, WolframModelPlot, Wolfram model rule, physics project hypergraph, QuantumToMultiwaySystem, metamathematics, proof graph, FindEquationalProof, Gorard, Piskunov, Murzin, Arsiwalla, Elshatlawy, Namuduri, token event graph, causal graph foliation, hypergraph dimension, combinator evolution, quantum framework multiway, WolframPhysicsProjectStyleData, MultiwayCombinator, HypergraphRewriting, Wolfram model 3D, branchial space, rulial space
- https://community.wolfram.com/u/search/users?term=NAME&limit=8 with the header Accept: application/json, for NAME in Gorard, Piskunov, Arsiwalla, Elshatlawy, Namuduri, "Stephen Wolfram", Hammock (without the header the endpoint returns the HTML shell). Usernames found: jonathangorard, jg865, jonathang, maxitg, maxp, piskunovm, xdarsiwalla, hatemelshatlawy, hatemelshatlawy1, hatem, manojna, manon, duganh; no account for Stephen Wolfram.
- https://community.wolfram.com/topics/created-by/USERNAME.json for murzinnikolay (the legacy profile URL https://community.wolfram.com/web/murzinnikolay redirects to https://community.wolfram.com/u/murzinnikolay) and for each username above, plus the guesses stephenwolfram and swolfram (no such users).
- curl -sI on the short links loaded by posts to resolve them: https://wolfr.am/KXgcRNRJ and https://wolfr.am/LcADnk1u (wolframphysics TechPaper-Programs Section-05 FoliationLines.wl and CausalFoliations-01.wl), https://wolfr.am/DevWQCF (QuantumFramework.paclet), https://wolfr.am/Multicomputation.paclet and https://www.wolfr.am/Hypergraph.paclet (nikm cloud objects), https://wolfr.am/PL39QRbE and https://wolfr.am/PL3LfaQ4 (sw-blog EuclidBlog Programs-04.wl and Programs-05.wl), https://wolfr.am/1z88Lc63z and https://wolfr.am/1z88b1Snz (ccn2 freeform init files), https://wolfr.am/nks-page0071a-graphic (nksonline graphic source, not a package).
- WebFetch probes: https://community.wolfram.com/content?curTag=wolfram%20physics%20project (HTTP 404) and https://community.wolfram.com/groups/-/m/t/2029319 (the summariser saw the post shell but not the notebook iframe content, which is why the JSON route was used).
- https://community.wolfram.com/t/ID.json for every candidate topic: 688 topics from the tag sweep and searches, 41 supplemental ids from the author searches and the physics and graphs-and-networks tag sweeps, and the two Jonathan Gorard topics, 731 topics in all. Each topic JSON carries the first post and the replies as "cooked" HTML. Wolfram Notebook posts are embedded as a server side rendered notebook inside an iframe srcdoc (class wolfram-notebook-ssr-container, data-notebook-url pointing at the source cloud object); Input and Code cells render as character spans in "lines" divs, Text cells as "native-layout" divs.

### Mining procedure (python scripts sweep_tag.py, search.py, fetch_topics.py, mine2.py, assemble.py in the scratchpad)

For every fetched topic the script extracted: the title, author, date and tags; every fenced code block and every notebook Input or Code cell of the first post and of the replies; every ResourceFunction["Name"] reference; every PacletSymbol, Needs, Get, PacletInstall and << reference; every definition of the form name[pattern args] := or = at the start of a line; and every CamelCase symbol applied with [ , @, /@ or // that is not a `` System` `` symbol (`` System` `` names exported from the kernel with `` Names["System`*"] ``, 7934 names in WL 15.0). For each definition it also captured the inline comment and the text cell preceding the code cell, which is the "description in the words of the source" used in the tables. Topic relevance was decided from the title (keywords such as multiway, hypergraph, Wolfram model, causal, branchial, rulial, ruliad, combinator, rewriting, substitution system, metamathematics) or from the use of Physics Project Function Repository functions or SetReplace.

### Kernel evaluations (wolframscript -file, WL 15.0)

- `` Names["System`*"] `` exported to sysnames.txt (7934 names).
- For every Function Repository name referenced by the posts (and every unexplained CamelCase name used by them): ResourceObject[name]["Description"], ["FunctionInformation"] usage, ["ContributorInformation"] and ["Keywords"], wrapped in TimeConstrained[..., 120]. Names for which ResourceObject failed are recorded as MISSING; those are either package or paclet functions or private definitions that the post did not show.


## Findings

Topics fetched: 731. Topics judged in scope for the Physics Project: 436, of which 106 are application-domain multiway posts (games, puzzles, linguistics, chemistry, music). Function Repository functions referenced: 138. Post-defined functions found: 1615; the 955 from the core posts are tabulated below and the 660 from the application-domain posts are listed by name inline under their posts.

Source column key: WFR is the Wolfram Function Repository; Community means the function is defined in the community post itself (the URL is the post); Paclet names the paclet; WL is a built-in.

### Highlights: posts that ship reusable tooling

These are the posts a paclet author should read first. Each line gives the post, the author, the date, and the tooling in the author's words.

- Wolfram Model Explorer by Zolmeister Zman (2020-05-26), https://community.wolfram.com/t/16082: a web explorer, source at https://github.com/Zolmeister/wolfram-model-explorer (JavaScript, not Wolfram Language).
- Wolfram Model Tool, Explorer and 3d Videos of Wolfram Models by Tuomas Sorakivi (2020-08-17), https://community.wolfram.com/t/16588: "a new open listing of all universe models explored in 3d" (Hypergraph Universes, https://github.com/gigabrainIO/HypergraphUniverses), with follow-ups 3D printing Wolfram Models (https://community.wolfram.com/t/17535) and "Wolfram Model and hypergraph search API released" (https://community.wolfram.com/t/18258), "a new beta test application interface to search for Wolfram Models and Hypergraphs based on search criteria as an external gigabrain.io API service".
- A PAOH hypergraph rendering for Wolfram Models in fundamental physics by Marcos Ferrer (2020-05-31), https://community.wolfram.com/t/16118, and PAOH Hypergraphs for Wolfram Models (https://community.wolfram.com/t/16167): parallel aggregated ordered hypergraph renderings built on WolframModelData; code lives in the linked cloud notebooks.
- [WSS20] Multi perspectives, 3D graph embeddings and multispace by Nikolay Murzin (2020-07-13), https://community.wolfram.com/t/16320: the origin of the Function Repository functions MultiPerspectiveEmbedding, MultiPerspectiveEmbeddingViewer and MultispacePlot3D, applied to MultiwaySystem and MultiwayTuringMachine output.
- [WSS21] Plotting the evolution of a Wolfram Model in 3-dimensions by Dugan Hammock (2021-07-14), https://community.wolfram.com/t/18490: defines wmEventsPlot3D[evolution_WolframModelEvolutionObject, options___].
- [WSS22] Temporally coherent animations of the evolution of Wolfram Models by Dugan Hammock (2022-07-20), https://community.wolfram.com/t/20366: "a novel method for plotting and animating the evolutions of spatial hypergraphs for Wolfram Models"; defines WolframModelEventsPlot3D[evolution_WolframModelEvolutionObject, ...].
- [WSS23] Animating Wolfram Model evolutions in 3D by Dugan Hammock (2023-07-12), https://community.wolfram.com/t/22383: uses WolframModelAnimation2D, WolframModelAnimation2DStackPlot and WolframModelAnimation3D from a closed "Included Packages and Functions" group (definitions not visible; unconfirmed).
- [WSRP24] HyperPlot: on the generation of temporally coherent hypergraphs by Gregory Roudenko (2024-07-11), https://community.wolfram.com/t/24285: a layout for animating hypergraph growth that fixes the problem that spring-electrical plots "do not use any information from previous graphs", on top of SetReplace; 21 functions defined (getElectricForces, getSpringForces, updatePositions, addNewVertices, iterate, ...).
- Comparing common dimension estimators for hypergraph by Rauan Kaldybaev (2021-03-31), https://community.wolfram.com/t/17907: compares the Myrheim-Meyer, Wolfram-Hausdorff and mean-shortest-path estimators (mmDim, whDim, msDim, mmRob, whDiffDist, ...) on registry models wm2224, wm1268, wm3255, wm1811.
- [WSS20] Hunting for black holes by Carlos Rafael Garduno Acolt (2020-07-14), https://community.wolfram.com/t/16372: blackHoleQ, causalConnectionGraph, blackHoleCausalPlot, blackHoleSpatialPlot on top of CausalConnectionGraph; continued in Black Hole Discussion (https://community.wolfram.com/t/17685).
- [WWS21] Analyzing Effects of Dimensional Perturbations in Wolfram Models by Santiago Giner (2021-01-14), https://community.wolfram.com/t/17414: CausalDiamondDimensionEstimator, FindEndEvents, ResultingDimensionOfRule, GraphSubregionDimensionEstimator.
- [WSS22] Compiling different models of computation into hypergraph rewriting by Utkarsh Bajaj (2022-07-19), https://community.wolfram.com/t/20343: DFAtoHypergraph, TuringToHypergraph, RMtoHypergraph and helpers that compile automata, Turing machines and register machines into Wolfram model rules, with FindWolframModelProof.
- [WSS21] A topological introduction to causal set theory and Wolfram Physics by Rafael Turner (2021-07-14), https://community.wolfram.com/t/18486: 33 causal-set functions (Hasse diagrams, intervals, sprinklings) next to MultiwaySystem and WolframModelPlot.
- [WSS21] Coarse-graining state networks with approximate graph isomorphism by Jessica Yuan (2021-07-14), https://community.wolfram.com/t/18488: 27 functions on canonical hypergraphs and enumeration (CanonicalHypergraph, EnumerateHypergraphs, EnumerateWolframModelRules).
- [WWS22] Identifying homogeneity in hypergraphs via approximate isomorphism by Regen Petu-Stiles (2022-01-14), https://community.wolfram.com/t/19410: 19 functions on FlatManifoldToGraph, GraphNeighborhoodVolumes and HypergraphToGraph.
- [FSWS23] What should space look like in the Wolfram Model? by Lev Chizhov (2023-01-12), https://community.wolfram.com/t/21398: 25 functions around FindCanonicalWolframModel and FindGroupIsomorphism.
- Branchial wormholes by Nikolay Murzin (2022-07-21), https://community.wolfram.com/t/20377, String rewriting trace graphs by Nikolay Murzin (2024-05-08), https://community.wolfram.com/t/23967, and Quantum operators from Wolfram Model multiway systems by Furkan Semih Dundar (2026-01-01), https://community.wolfram.com/t/26284: all load the Wolfram/Multicomputation paclet (https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/nikm/Multicomputation.paclet) and use MultiwaySystem, HypergraphMulti, ToLinkedHypergraph and FromLinkedHypergraph from it; PathTraceGraph is defined in the trace graphs post itself (PathTraceGraph[path_] := Block[...], verified 2026-09-16).
- [WSS23] The Ruliology of Network Mobile Automata by Joel Choi (2023-07-12), https://community.wolfram.com/t/22389, [WSS23] Studying behavior of simple network rewriting system by Minaya Allahverdiyeva (https://community.wolfram.com/t/22397) and [WSRP25] Visualizing particle decay chains by Julia Gao (https://community.wolfram.com/t/25728): load the WolframInstitute/Hypergraph paclet (https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/nikm/Hypergraph.paclet) and use Hypergraph, HypergraphRule and CanonicalHypergraphRule.
- [WWS21] Relativistic mass transformations from simple causal graph by Tom Lee (https://community.wolfram.com/t/17402), Douglas Hofstadter's GEB MU puzzle in the Wolfram Model by Alisson Silva (https://community.wolfram.com/t/19484), [WSS24] The equivalence principle, hyperbolic geometry and gyrogeometry by Ibrahim Bukvic (https://community.wolfram.com/t/24196) and [WSS24] On motion and special relativity in Wolfram Models by Lev Chizhov (https://community.wolfram.com/t/24206): load the Technical Introduction programs FoliationLines.wl and CausalFoliations-01.wl from https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/wolframphysics/TechPaper-Programs/Section-05/.
- [WSS21] Computational complexity classes, homotopy classes, and n-machines by James Boyd (https://community.wolfram.com/t/18458) and [FSWS23] Musical Composition with Multiway Turing Machines by Joel Dietz (https://community.wolfram.com/t/21415): load https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/sw-blog/MultiwayTuringMachines/Programs-01.wl, the programs from Stephen Wolfram's multiway Turing machines essay.
- Code that goes with "Patterns in Combinator Evolution" paper by Eric Parfitt (2020-11-25), https://community.wolfram.com/t/17101, Another way to make graphs out of combinator expressions (https://community.wolfram.com/t/17100), Evaluating combinators with StringReplace (https://community.wolfram.com/t/15767) and Decompiling combinators to lambda calculus (https://community.wolfram.com/t/17815): combinator tooling by the same author (18 lambda-calculus functions in the last one).
- [WSC20] Analyzing and computing with combinators by Eric Paul (2020-07-15), https://community.wolfram.com/t/16420: CombinatorReplace, CombinatorEvaluate, CombinatorEvaluateAll, CombinatorEvaluateAllSteps, LambdaToSK, Church numeral encoders.
- [WSRP24] Monoids, string-rewriting, confluence, and the Knuth-Bendix Algorithm by Vivaan Daga (2024-07-12), https://community.wolfram.com/t/24312: "We implement the Knuth-Bendix Algorithm for monoids in Wolfram Language" (knuthBendixComp, resolveoverlays, reduce, ...).
- On the optimal move problem for multiway systems by Navvye Anand (2024-04-26), https://community.wolfram.com/t/23917: 13 functions around CausallyInvariantQ, KnuthBendixCompletion and MultiwaySystem.
- Square Grid Universe: programming one as a hypergraph rewriting ruleset by Myk Konovalow (2023-11-15), https://community.wolfram.com/t/23106: a rule-construction toolkit (OUTER, CORNER, RIM, MERG, DefineMarker, ...) for building a rewriting ruleset that grows a square grid.
- Enumerating local rewriting rules for two-link network systems by Brian A. Mboya (2025-03-20), https://community.wolfram.com/t/25285: enumerates NKS network-system rules (GetDistanceDRules, TableDisplay are used but defined in the attached notebook).
- Exploring the space of interaction nets by Eric Parfitt (2025-08-11), https://community.wolfram.com/t/25866: interaction nets as a graph rewriting language, using OrderedGraphModelPlot and WolframModel.
- [WSS19] Confluent Set Substitution Systems by Max Piskunov (2019-07-10), https://community.wolfram.com/t/14367: the SetReplace era post (SetReplace, SetSubstitutionSystem, HypergraphPlot; https://github.com/maxitg/SetReplace).
- [WSS17] Automated Theorem Proving for Equational Logic by Jonathan Gorard (2017-07-05), https://community.wolfram.com/t/10788: the project that extended the FindEquationalProof backend (code at https://github.com/GetJonWithIt/starthere).

### Posts by the named authors

Usernames found with https://community.wolfram.com/u/search/users and topics listed with https://community.wolfram.com/topics/created-by/USERNAME.json.

- Stephen Wolfram: no personal account posts; his material appears through the Wolfram Community staff account (for example https://community.wolfram.com/t/28040) and through the Programs .wl files loaded by student posts.
- Max Piskunov (maxitg): [WSS19] Confluent Set Substitution Systems, https://community.wolfram.com/t/14367.
- Jonathan Gorard (jonathangorard): [WSS17] Automated Theorem Proving for Equational Logic, https://community.wolfram.com/t/10788; Announcing the Wolfram Summer School Fundamental Physics Track (2020-04-21), https://community.wolfram.com/t/15819. He is named as mentor in more than 100 project posts (search "Gorard" returned 123 topics).
- Xerxes Arsiwalla (xdarsiwalla): [WSS20] Homotopic foundations of Wolfram Models, https://community.wolfram.com/t/16400 (no code shown); mentor on 82 topics by search.
- Nikolay Murzin (murzinnikolay): [WSS20] Multi perspectives, 3D graph embeddings and multispace (https://community.wolfram.com/t/16320), Branchial wormholes (https://community.wolfram.com/t/20377), String rewriting trace graphs (https://community.wolfram.com/t/23967), plus Wolfram Quantum Framework posts (On quantum amplitudes, correlations and negativity, https://community.wolfram.com/t/22905; Bohmian quantum computation, https://community.wolfram.com/t/24335; Simulating hybrid Classical-Quantum trajectories, https://community.wolfram.com/t/25415) that are outside the Physics Project scope of this sweep.
- Hatem Elshatlawy (hatemelshatlawy): [WSS20] Investigating quantum interference effects in Wolfram Models, https://community.wolfram.com/t/16341 (allTrajectories, allTrajectoriesNR); mentor on 41 topics by search.
- Mano Namuduri (manojna): [WSS20] Comparing expansion in physical and branchial space, https://community.wolfram.com/t/16361 (code rendered as images; no definitions visible); mentor on 18 topics by search.
- Dugan Hammock (duganh): the three Wolfram model animation posts listed under Highlights.

## Gaps

- The legacy group URL https://community.wolfram.com/content?curTag=wolfram%20physics%20project returns 404 after the migration to Discourse, and there is no "wolfram-physics-project" tag in https://community.wolfram.com/tags.json. The sweep used the "ruliology" tag (892 topics), the "physics" tag (2462 topics) and the "graphs-and-networks" tag (1338 topics) plus full text search instead. Posts that were in the old group but are now tagged only with another tag and whose titles contain none of the search terms would be missed.
- Discourse search.json returned HTTP 429 (Too Many Requests) on the first pass for 17 of the 33 queries (two of them after returning partial pages); those queries were rerun with a 4 second delay and all completed. The sweep script fetched at most 8 pages (50 topics each) per query; the queries "Wolfram Physics Project", "Wolfram model rule", "proof graph" and "combinator evolution" filled all 8 pages (400 topics), so lower ranked matches for those queries were not enumerated.
- Many school project posts embed the notebook as a server side rendered iframe. Cells that the cloud renders as images (typeset output, some Code cells, closed cell groups such as "Included Packages and Functions") are not text, so their definitions are invisible. 125 function names are therefore recorded as used but unconfirmed (166 before the verification pass, see Verification), and posts such as [WSS20] Comparing expansion in physical and branchial space (t/16361) and [WSS20] Classify growth rates for Wolfram Models (t/16338) show usage of WolframModel and EnumerateWolframModelRules only.
- The linked source notebooks (https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/... in each post, and the wolframphysics.org Programs .wl files) were not opened; only the rendered post content was mined. Opening each cloud notebook would recover the hidden definitions.
- Replies were mined for ResourceFunction names and definitions but the reply threads of very long topics are paginated by Discourse (only the first 20 posts come with /t/ID.json); later replies were not fetched.
- Posts before April 2020 (NKS-era ruliology) are included only when they matched the search terms; the full ruliology tag before 2020 was listed but not fetched except for the keyword matches.
- The WebFetch tool sees the post page without the notebook iframe content, so it was only used for the first probe; all content came from the JSON endpoints.
- ResourceObject lookups ran once per name on 2026-09-16; 376 names used in posts had no Function Repository entry (MISSING), which is expected for post-local names but may also hide repository functions that were renamed.

## Verification

Independent check of every row of the findings tables on 2026-09-16 (1287 rows: 138 WFR, 955 Community, 151 Community (undefined in post), 28 Package or paclet, 15 Paclet). Methods:

- WFR rows (138): one scratch script (wfr_check.wls, WL 15.0 kernel, wolframscript -file) evaluated `Quiet@ResourceObject[name]` with `["ResourceType"]`, `["Description"]` and `["ContributorInformation"]` for every name, each wrapped in TimeConstrained[..., 60]. All 138 returned ResourceType Function and a description identical to the one in the table. Every repository URL was also fetched over HTTP (curl, all 138 returned 200 with the page title "Name | Wolfram Function Repository"; a missing name redirects to the Wolfram Account sign-in page, so the title is the discriminating check). Five random rows (CanonicalWolframModelRule, ParallelMapMonitored, FindCanonicalHypergraph, CoprimeIntegerList, NestWhileGraph) were additionally read with WebFetch and their name, description and contributor matched the table.
- Community rows (955), Community (undefined in post) rows (151), Package or paclet rows (28) and Paclet rows (15): every cited topic (177 topics) was re-fetched live from https://community.wolfram.com/t/ID.json, the cooked HTML and the server side rendered notebook iframes (srcdoc, character spans) were flattened to text, and each name was searched for as a whole token. For every Community row the signature in the Description column (whitespace removed) or a definition of the form name[...] := or name = was found in the post. For every undefined-in-post row the name is applied in the post. For every Package or paclet row the package context, paclet name or file URL appears in the cited post; the wolfr.am short links the posts actually load were resolved with curl -sI and redirect (301) to the URLs given in the table. All 1149 rows were found.
- Paclet rows (15) and paclet exports: the symbols were confirmed as exported by grepping PackageExport lines in the installed paclets WolframInstitute/Hypergraph 1.2.3, Wolfram/Multicomputation 0.1.9 and Wolfram/QuantumFramework 2.1.1 (under the local Paclets/Repository, no new install was needed) and in the IGraphM sources fetched from https://raw.githubusercontent.com/szhorvat/IGraphM/master/IGraphM/ (Cliques.m, Matching.m, GraphMotifs.m, Utilities.m, Isomorphism.m). All 15 are exported; their Status was changed from unconfirmed to seen and the source file is named in each row.
- All 166 undefined-in-post and paclet names were looked up with ResourceObject in a second scratch script (names_check.wls); none is on the Function Repository, so no row needed re-sourcing to WFR. The same script checked every Community-defined name against `` Names["System`" <> name] `` in WL 15.0: only IntersectingQ (t/25373, defined there as IntersectingQ[vox1_, vox2_]) collides with a built-in symbol of the same name, which is worth knowing before porting that code. The tables contain no WL rows, so no further WL check was needed.
- The 659 names listed inline as "defines (not tabulated)" under the application-domain posts (70 posts, all re-fetched) were checked the same way; all are defined in their posts. The Findings paragraph says 660.
- The four GitHub repositories named in Highlights returned HTTP 200.

Corrections made:

- 24 rows re-sourced from Community (undefined in post) to Community with Status seen, because the live post does show a definition that the mining pattern skipped (zero-argument definitions such as name[] :=, plain assignments such as name = value, subvalue definitions, a definition preceded by a non-breaking space, and definitions in replies): AdinkraManualGraph3D, AndCombinator, BHdimensionalities, BranchialEdge, C3OperationCombinator, CombinatorBooleanExample, DrawGraph, FoliationSets, GenerateMultiway, InitializeQuantumGrowth, MakeReversibleMobileAutomata, MultiwayTagExplorer, PathTraceGraph, QuantumFibonacciSystem, RegisterRule2, SpacetimeSprinkling, SpinstIs, SpinstNet, TrivalentMultiwayReductionRules, TrivalentReductionRules, TrivalentReductionRulesFromTetra, TrivalentSpinNetworkEvolution, TuringMachineCompiledSimple, XorCombinator. The seen definition is now the first item of each Description.
- 2 rows re-sourced from Community (undefined in post) to Paclet (Wolfram/Multicomputation, loaded by the post) with Status seen: FromLinkedHypergraph and ToLinkedHypergraph, both exported in Kernel/LinkedHypergraph.m and both used in t/23967, which loads the paclet.
- 15 Paclet rows: Status changed from unconfirmed to seen (exports confirmed as above).
- Row Hypergraph (Paclet WolframInstitute/Hypergraph): the URL was re-pointed from t/18488 to t/22389. The 2021 post t/18488 does not load the paclet; its Hypergraph is a local wrapper head (HypergraphEvolve[rule_, hypergraph_Hypergraph] := ...). The posts t/22389, t/24244 and t/25728 load the paclet and use the symbol.
- Highlights: the sentence on Branchial wormholes, String rewriting trace graphs and Quantum operators no longer lists PathTraceGraph as a Multicomputation paclet function; PathTraceGraph is not exported by Wolfram/Multicomputation 0.1.9 and is defined in the trace graphs post itself.
- The section intro for names without a visible definition and the Gaps bullet were updated to the new count of 125 unconfirmed rows.

Removed: none. Every row's name exists where the (corrected) row says it does.

Left unconfirmed: the remaining 125 Community (undefined in post) rows. Each name is applied in the cited post but its definition is not visible in the rendered post, it is not on the Function Repository (ResourceObject lookup failed on 2026-09-16), and where the name matches an export of a public paclet (BranchialGraph in Wolfram/Multicomputation, HypergraphAutomorphismGroup and SetReplace in SetReplace, HypergraphRule in WolframInstitute/Hypergraph, MaTeX in the MaTeX package) the cited post does not visibly load that paclet, so the attribution cannot be confirmed from the post.
